A win_prob of 0.26 was fair: the narrow defeat confirms real winning contention, not a false positive, and is aligned with morning/market pricing (~3.6).

Falcon Nine is the most solid profile on the brief: joint-top OR 58, proven CD (1-2), strong Good and 9f records (G 2-7, D 2-5), and draw 7 fits Musselburgh’s slight high-draw lean. Recent Ayr form is competitive rather than exposed — two close seconds at 10f on Good/G-F and a narrow ninth — and Shoemark/Jardine are the sharper half of a dual entry. He looks the most likely to run to his rating over this trip; the market favourite tag reflects suitability, not certainty.

The main doubt is finishing power in a tight handicap: he keeps finding trouble or one gear short of winning, and 26% is a long way below a 54% model read — in a seven-runner Class 6, no one should dominate. Miners Gamble is the main danger on CD 1-1, a 6lb weight pull, and draw 6, but slow-start history and recent AW form cap him; Samra Star brings fresh winning momentum but 0-5 on Good and draw 1 are negatives at this trip.

This is also a low-confidence 0-50 with several stale or AW-only profiles (Thank The Lord, Drish Samphire), so an imperfect favourite could still win if the race develops wrong tactically — hence a modest 15% true win estimate rather than a strong staking opinion.

Shes Got The Blues is the clearest form and suitability case: 1-1 at Brighton, 1-1 at 7f course-and-distance on Good To Firm, and two wins from three at today’s trip. She handles this track’s demands better than anyone else here, Tony Carroll’s yard is the strongest on the card, and draw 5 sits in the better half of Brighton’s high-draw 7f bias. King Cherry is the main danger on top weight and official rating—he reappears only 14 days after a close 1.2L seventh over this exact course, distance, going and class—but he is still 0-1 at Brighton and has not shown the winning habit Blues has.

The biggest risk to Blues is not ability but setup: 41 days off, a below-par Windsor run over 8f, and Myla Coppins carrying a claim with no recent winners. If Snow Day leads for the Carroll pair or the race develops into a strongly run contest favouring a high-draw late finisher, King Cherry or even Superstorm on draw 7 could outrun their broader form. Even so, on true winning chance rather than market rank, Blues leads a messy Class 6 handicap.

Casino Star’s Hamilton second reads well for trip and going but draw 2 and a 77 OR cap him; Dance A Jig has 5f placings but a long break and no Good-ground win; Rotelle’s Nottingham form is useful but draw 1 is a negative here; Undercover Affair gets the best stall but 0-4 on Good is hard to ignore. The biggest risk to God Given Talent is that the race may simply be too quick and competitive for a one-time 6f winner to dominate first time at five.
